and
H_{2}:=H
. Then (a) follows by
(ImpAnd)
, (b) and (c) are immediate.

(Scope)
:

In this case,
G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
has shape
\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
and
H
has shape
G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
. We assume
G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
does not contain any variable from
\overline{{x}}
. Hence, neither does
G
and so we can rewrite:

V,\overline{{y}}\overline{{z}}\vdash\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G\triangleright G

Hence, the result follows by taking
U:=\mathsf{true}
,
H_{1}:=\mathsf{true}
,
K:=G
and
H_{2}:=G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
.

∎

C.1.
Completeness

To show that
D\models G
implies
\mathcal{V}(D)\models G
, we will argue that one can use a proof of
D\vdash G
as a guide to follow in order to obtain exactly the automaton clauses
V\subseteq\mathcal{V}(D)
needed to replay the proof as
V\vdash G\triangleright\mathsf{true}
.
Clearly, the main aim of our argument will be to show that, wherever we made a resolution step using a clause from
D
in the proof of
D\vdash G
, we can find an automaton clause in
\mathcal{V}(D)
with which to simulate it when rewriting.

To see why, consider again the proof in Figure
4
.
We can observe that the resolution steps at the leaves of the proof necessarily involve an automaton clause as the side premise: it cannot be otherwise since a resolution step at a leaf must be via a clause that has a vacuous body, such as
\mathsf{true}\Rightarrow\mathsf{Closed}(\mathsf{c})
, and clauses of this form are necessarily automaton.

Then, there may be many consecutive resolution steps leading up to this leaf that all happen to involve clauses from
D
that are automaton already.
We observe that such a situation gives rise to a maximal subproof, say of
D\vdash H
which can be viewed equivalently as a proof
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash H
, since it uses only clauses that are automaton (and hence in
\mathcal{V}(D)
).
In the case of Figure
4
, the subproof rooted at
\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ (\mathsf{putCont}\ \mathsf{id}))
is of this form, since (18) and (7) both happen to be automaton.

If the subproof
D\vdash H
is the largest that contains only automaton inferences, then continuing down the proof tree, the next resolution step involves a clause from
D
that is not itself automaton.
Let us say that this inference is of shape:

\displaystyle{D\vdash G^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{y}}]}\mathstrut

(Res)

(\forall\overline{{y}}.\ G^{\prime}\Rightarrow A)\in D

\displaystyle D\vdash A[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{y}}]\mathstrut

and, for simplicity, that
H=G^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{y}}]
.
The fact that
\forall\overline{{y}}.\ G^{\prime}\Rightarrow A
is not automaton indicates that
G^{\prime}
is not of the correct form.
However, the subproof
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash H
that sits on top of this inference shows us how to prove
G^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{y}}]
using only resolution with automaton clauses.
We can view this proof as a specification for rewriting
G^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{y}}]
to
\mathsf{true}
, we just need to perform the same resolution steps in the same order.
The following lemma shows that performing the same rewriting sequence on the raw goal
G^{\prime}
(which is the body that is preventing the
(Res)
inference from being automaton) will give rise to an automaton formula.

Lemma C.3 (Decomposition).

If
V\vdash G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
then there is some
U
such that:

(a)

V,\,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U

(b)

and
V\vdash U[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}

Proof.

By induction on the length
n
of the rewrite sequence
V\vdash G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{n}\mathsf{true}
.

•

When
n=1
,
G=\mathsf{true}
and so we can take
U:=\mathsf{true}
too.

•

Otherwise, the sequence has shape
V\vdash G[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright H\triangleright^{n-1}\mathsf{true}
and we can invoke progress (Lemma
C.2
) to obtain some
U^{\prime}
and
K
such that (a)
V\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}\wedge K
and we can split
H
as
H_{1}\wedge H_{2}
with
V\vdash U^{\prime}[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}H_{1}
and
K[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]=H_{2}
. Since
V\vdash H\triangleright^{n-1}\mathsf{true}
, there are sequences
V\vdash H_{1}\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
and
V\vdash H_{2}\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
of length at most
n-1
. So, by the induction hypothesis, we obtain some
U^{\prime\prime}
such that
V\vdash K\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime\prime}
and
V\vdash U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
. Hence, we choose
U:=U^{\prime}\wedge U^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

For example, in Figure
4
, although the resolution step rooted at
\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{putStrLn}\ \mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ \mathsf{id})
uses clause (10), namely
\forall x\,h\,k.\ \mathsf{V}(x\ h\ (\mathsf{putCont}\ k))\Rightarrow\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{putStrLn}\ x\ h\ k)
, the proof above this point shows us that resolving with automaton clauses (7) and (18) allows for rewriting
\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ (\mathsf{putCont}\ \mathsf{id}))
to
\mathsf{true}
:

\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ (\mathsf{putCont}\ \mathsf{id}))\triangleright\mathsf{Closed}(\mathsf{c})\triangleright\mathsf{true}

Using
(Assm)
, we can simulate these same resolution steps on the raw body of clause (10) to obtain a new automaton formula:

\mathsf{V}(x\ h\ (\mathsf{putCont}\ k))\triangleright\mathsf{Closed}(h)\wedge(\forall h^{\prime}\,k^{\prime}.\ \mathsf{Closed}(h^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathsf{V}(x\ h^{\prime}\ k^{\prime}))

Here, we are adding the assumption that
x
behaves like
\mathsf{read}
does, according to clause (7).
This then justifies adding a new automaton clause to
\mathcal{V}(D)
corresponding to (10):

\forall x\,h\,k.\ \mathsf{Closed}(h)\wedge(\forall h^{\prime}\,k^{\prime}.\ \mathsf{Closed}(h^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathsf{V}(x\ h^{\prime}\ k^{\prime}))\Rightarrow\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{putStrLn}\ x\ h\ k)

This new clause allows us to extend our subproof now one further deduction, to:

D\vdash\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{putStrLn}\ \mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ \mathsf{id})

This reflects that
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash\mathsf{V}(\mathsf{putStrLn}\ \mathsf{read}\ \mathsf{c}\ \mathsf{id})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
By repeating this process we can replay the whole deduction using only automaton clauses from
\mathcal{V}(D)
. Hence:

See
4.7

Proof.

We show
D\vdash G
implies
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash G\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
by induction on the proof
D\vdash G
. The result then follows from soundness (Theorem
4.5
).

(True)
:

The result is immediate.

(Res)
:

In this case,
G
is of shape
P\ s[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
. The premise is
D\vdash H[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]
for some
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,H\Rightarrow P\ s)\in D
. It follows from the induction hypothesis that
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash H[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
. Hence, by decomposition, there is some
U
such that
\mathcal{V}(D),\,\overline{{y}}\vdash H\triangleright^{*}U
and
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash U[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
. Then, by definition,
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\ s)\in\mathcal{V}(D)
and so we can use
(Step)
to rewrite
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash P\ s[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright U[\overline{{u}}/\overline{{y}}]\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
, as required.

(And)
:

In this case,
G
is of shape
H_{1}\wedge H_{2}
and it follows from the induction hypothesis that
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash H_{1}\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
and
\mathcal{V}(D)\vdash H_{2}\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
. Hence, the result follows straightforwardly.

∎

C.2.
Decidability

Theorem C.4 (Decidability).

Let
V
be an automaton formula,
\overline{y}
is a sequence of variables,
G
an arbitary goal formula and
U
an automaton formula.
Then
V,\,\overline{y}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U
is decidable.

Proof.

Definition C.5.

The
weight
of a clause is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(\textsf{true})

\displaystyle\coloneqq 0

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(P\ t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 0pt{t}

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(G\wedge H)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathsf{weight}(G)+\mathsf{weight}(H)

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow\textsf{true})=1

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{x}.\,U\Rightarrow P\ t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}0pt{t}&\textrm{if}\ \mathsf{FV}(t)\cap\{x\}\neq\emptyset\\
1+0pt{t}&\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{x}.\,U\Rightarrow H_{1}\wedge H_{2})

\displaystyle\coloneq 1+\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow H_{1})+\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow H_{2})

\displaystyle\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{x}.\,U\Rightarrow(\forall\overline{y}.\,V\Rightarrow G))

\displaystyle\coloneq 1+\mathsf{weight}(\forall\overline{y}.\,V\Rightarrow G)

When multiple cases apply due to the quotient structure of conjunction, we take the smallest weight.

Lemma C.6.

If
\mathsf{measure}(G)<\mathsf{measure}(G^{\prime})
, then
\mathsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.U\Rightarrow G)<\mathsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.U\Rightarrow G^{\prime})
.

Lemma C.7.

If
U
is an automaton formula, then the weight of
U[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]
is at most
\max\{0pt{s}\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
.

Proof.

In the case of
(P\ x)[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]
, we have that weight is bounded by
\max\{0pt{s}\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
as
x
may be instantiated with any
\overline{{s}}
.
For
(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\ (x\ \overline{{z}}))[\overline{{s}}/xx]
, we have weight
0pt{s}\leq\max\{0pts\mid\in\overline{{s}}\}
as
U
only contains the variables
\overline{{z}}
.
Otherwise,
U
is a conjunction of both cases and we can proceed by structural induction.
∎

Lemma C.8.

Let
V
be an automaton formula,
\overline{y}
is a sequence of variables, and
G
and
H
formulas such that
V,\,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright H
.
Then
\mathsf{weight}(H)<\mathsf{weight}(G)
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
V,\,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright H
:

(Refl)
:

Trivial.

(Step)
:

This case
G
is the atom
P\ (f\ \overline{{s}})
and has depth
1+\max\{0pts\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
.
By the previous lemma, the weight of
U[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]
is at most
\max\{0pts\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
as it is an automaton body.
Therefore, we have a proper decrease as required.

(Assm)
:

In this case,
G
is the atom
P\ (x\ ss)
which has depth
1+\max\{0pts\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
and the reduct is
U[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{z}}]\wedge(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\ (x\ \overline{{z}}))
which has depth
\max\{0pt{s}\mid s\in\overline{{s}}\}
, again by the previous lemma.
Thus this case is also satisfied.

(AndL)
,
(AndR)
:

Trivial.

(Imp)
:

In this case, the goal is
\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G
where
G
is not of the form
P\ (y\ \overline{{x}})
.
By induction on
V\wedge U,\,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright G^{\prime}
, we have that
\mathsf{weight}(G^{\prime})<\mathsf{weight}(G)
.
Therefore,
\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G)<\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G^{\prime})
as required.

(ImpAnd)
:

Trivial.

(Scope)
:

In this case, the goal is
\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G
where
\mathsf{FV}{G}\cap\overline{{x}}=\emptyset
.
Consider inductively the possible shapes of
G
:

•:

\mathsf{true}
— the measure is
1
and after rewritting is
0
.

•:

P\ t
— the measure is
1+0pt{t}
and after rewriting is
1
.

•:

G_{1}\wedge G_{2}
— the measure is
1+\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G_{1})+\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U\Rightarrow G_{2})>1+\textsf{measure}(G_{1})+\textsf{measure}(G_{2})
and after rewritting it is just
\textsf{measure}(G_{1})+\textsf{measure}(G_{2})
.

•:

Finally, for
\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G
, the measure is
1+\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G)
and after rewriting it is
\textsf{measure}(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G)
.

In each case, we have a strict decrease as required.

∎

Corollary C.9.

For any choice of
V
,
\overline{y}
and
G
, there are finitely many reducts
G^{\prime}
such that
V,\,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright G^{\prime}
.

∎

Appendix D
Supporting Material for Section
5

D.1.
Types

Subtyping rules.

(Q-Bas)
:

if
(q_{1},q_{2})\in\Theta
, then
q_{1}\leq q_{2}

(Q-Arr)
:

if
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{2}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}
and
\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, then
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\tau_{1}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{2}\to\tau_{2}

(Q-Fun)
:

if
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\tau
, then
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}({\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau_{i})\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau

(Q-Prj)
:

for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}\tau_{j}\leq\tau_{i}

(Q-Glb)
:

if, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\leq\tau_{i}
, then
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\leq\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}\tau_{j}

(Q-Trs)
:

if
\theta_{1}\leq\theta_{2}
and
\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{3}
, then
\theta_{1}\leq\theta_{3}

Lemma D.1.

\textstyle\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{j}^{\prime}\quad\iff\quad\forall j\in[1..m].\,\exists i_{j}\in[1..n].\,\tau_{i_{j}}\leq\tau_{j}^{\prime}

Proof.

Direction
\Leftarrow
follows from
(Q-Glb)
followed by
(Q-Trs)
and
(Q-Prj)
.

For
\Rightarrow
, suppose
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{j}^{\prime}
.
We use induction on the subtyping rules, of which
(Q-Bas)
,
(Q-Prj)
, and
(Q-Arr)
are immediate.

(Q-Fun)
:

Here,
j=1
and the assumption is some
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}({\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau_{i})\leq({\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau^{\prime})
.
The premise is
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\tau^{\prime}
.
By the IH, there exists
i\in[1..n]
such that
\tau_{i}\leq\tau^{\prime}
.
It follows from
(Q-Arr)
that
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau_{i}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\to\tau^{\prime}
, which proves the claim.

(Q-Glb)
:

The premises are
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\tau_{j}^{\prime}
, for all
j\in[1..m]
.
By the IH, there exists
i_{j}\in[1..n]
such that
\tau_{i_{j}}\leq\tau_{j}^{\prime}
, for all
j\in[1..m]
, as required.

(Q-Trs)
:

The premises give us
\theta
such that
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}\leq\theta
and
\theta\leq\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{j}^{\prime}
.
Let
j\in[1..m]
.
By the IH, there exists
\tau^{\prime\prime}_{k}\in\theta
such that
\tau^{\prime\prime}_{k}\leq\tau^{\prime}_{j}
.
Furthermore, there exists
i_{k}\in[1..n]
such that
\tau_{i_{k}}\leq\tau^{\prime\prime}_{k}
.
By transitivity,
\tau_{i_{k}}\leq\tau^{\prime}_{j}
, as required.

∎

Lemma D.2 (Weakening).

If
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}
and
\overline{{z}}\cap\mathsf{FV}(t)=\emptyset
, then
\Gamma,{\overline{{z}}}::{\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}}\vdash{t}::{\tau}
for all
\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}
of the appropriate types.
Idem for
{G}::{\top_{o}}
.

D.2.
Typing-rewriting correspondence

Lemma D.3.

If
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow G_{1}\land G_{2})\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
, then there exist
U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime}
such that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow G_{1})\triangleright^{*}U_{1}^{\prime}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow G_{2})\triangleright^{*}U_{2}^{\prime}
and
U^{\prime}=U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}
.

Lemma D.4.

For automaton formulas
U
and
U^{\prime}
over
\overline{{z}}
,
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
if and only if
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove the claim by induction on automaton clause
U^{\prime}
:
an outer induction on the types of the variables in
U^{\prime}
and an inner induction on the number of conjuncts.
First, note that both
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
hold for
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{true}
.

Proof of direction
\Rightarrow
:

•

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,z_{i})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
This rewrite must start with
(Imp)
with
(Refl)
, which has
P\,z_{i}\in U
as side condition.
It is immediate that
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{P\,z_{i}}_{|{z_{i}}}})
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}P\,z_{i}
follows.

•

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
for
U_{1}^{\prime},U_{2}^{\prime}
containing only
z_{i}
of type
\iota
(and at least one such
z_{i}
each).
By Lemma
D.3
, we may assume WLOG that this rewrite starts with
(ImpAnd)
, i.e.
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime})\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
Thanks to
(AndL)
and
(AndR)
,
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
We apply the IH for
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}^{\prime}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2}^{\prime}
.
Clearly, also
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}
.

•

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}})))\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
for some
\overline{{x}}=x_{1}\dots x_{m}
.

–

Suppose this rewrite starts with
(Imp)
.
The only option for the premise is another
(Imp)
with
V\land U\land U^{\prime\prime},\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))\triangleright U^{\prime\prime\prime}
as premise.
We proceed as in the previous subcase.

•

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
Again, Lemma
D.3
allows us to assume WLOG that this rewrite starts with
(ImpAnd)
.
We proceed as in the first-order case.

Proof of direction
\Leftarrow
:

•

Suppose
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}P\,z_{i}
.
This means that
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq q_{P}
and, thus,
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})
and
P\,z_{i}\in U
.
We prove
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,z_{i})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
by
(Imp)
with
(Refl)
, followed by
(Scope)
.

•

Suppose
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}
containing only
z_{i}
of type
\iota
(and at least one such
z_{i}
each).
This means that
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({(U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})_{|{z_{i}}}})
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
Clearly, also
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{1}}^{\prime}_{|{z_{i}}}})
and
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{2}}^{\prime}_{|{z_{i}}}})
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
We now apply the IH to
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}^{\prime}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2}^{\prime}
for
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
This gives us a rewrite
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime})\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
to which we prepend an
(ImpAnd)
-step for
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.

•

Suppose
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))
for some
\overline{{x}}=x_{1}\dots x_{m}
.
This means
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})
.
By Lemma
D.1
, there exists
(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))\in U_{|{z_{i}}}
such that
\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})
.

This gives us
\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})
, for all
j\in[1..m]
.
This implies
U^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime\prime}
, to which we can apply the IH for
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
, because the types of
\overline{{x}}
are smaller than the type of
z_{i}
.
Trivially, also
V\land U,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.

We prepend
(Step)
with side condition
(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))\in U
to this rewrite to obtain
V\land U,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))\triangleright(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime\prime})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
We replicate this rewrite under an implication using
(Imp)
and conclude it with
(Scope)
:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(z_{i}\,\overline{{x}})))\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow\mathsf{true})\triangleright\mathsf{true}

This proves the case.

•

Suppose
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}
.
As the inductive first-order case.

∎

Lemma D.5 (Monotonicity of rewrite).

For all terms
t:\sigma_{1}\to\dots\to\sigma_{k}\to\iota
over
\overline{{y}}
, if
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U
, then there exists
U^{\prime}
such that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{i}
and
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{j}^{\prime}
such that
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U
.
By Subtyping Lemma
D.1
,
\forall j\in[1..m].\,\exists i_{j}\in[1..n].\,\tau_{i_{j}}\leq\tau_{j}^{\prime}
.
Clearly,
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}={\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime\prime}\land\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{i_{j}}
for some
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime\prime}
and, thus,
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})=\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime\prime}})({t})\land\mathsf{toCl}({\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})
.

This allows us to derive separate rewrites
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{1}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2}
such that
U=U_{1}\land U_{2}
from assumption
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U
.
Furthermore,
\mathsf{toCl}({\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})=\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{1}}})({t})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{m}}})({t})
allows us to separate the latter rewrite into
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}
, for all
j\in[1..m]
, such that
U_{2}=U_{2,1}\land\dots\land U_{2,m}
.

We aim to show that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}
determines a
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{j}^{\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}^{\prime}
with
U_{2,j}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2,j}^{\prime}
, for all
j\in[1..m]
.
This suffices due to
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}})({t})=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{j}^{\prime}})({t})
and:

U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2}=\textstyle\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}U_{2,j}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m}U_{2,j}^{\prime}

We use case analysis on the type of
t
.

Suppose
t:\iota
.
We only have trivial subtyping for type
\iota
, so
\forall j\in[1..m].\,\exists i_{j}\in[1..n].\,\tau_{i_{j}}=\tau_{j}^{\prime}
.
The claim is immediate.

Suppose
t:\sigma_{1}\to\dots\to\sigma_{k}\to\iota
for
k>0
.
Fix a
j\in[1..m]
with a corresponding
i_{j}\in[1..n]
.
Let
\tau_{i_{j}}=\theta_{1}\to\dots\to\theta_{k}\to q_{P}
and
\tau_{j}^{\prime}=\theta_{1}^{\prime}\to\dots\to\theta_{k}^{\prime}\to q_{P}
.
We know from
\tau_{i_{j}}\leq\tau_{j}^{\prime}
that
\theta_{\ell}^{\prime}\leq\theta_{\ell}
, for all
\ell\in[1..k]
.

Consider the rewrite
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}
we obtained from assumption
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U
;
since
\tau_{i_{j}}=\theta_{1}\to\dots\to\theta_{k}\to q_{P}
, it is equal to:

(†)

\displaystyle V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}

We proceed by case analysis on the length of this rewrite
†
‣
D.2
.

•

Suppose rewrite
†
‣
D.2
has length 0.
Then,
U_{2,j}=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))
is automaton wrt
\overline{{y}}
.
Clearly,
(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))
is also automaton wrt
\overline{{y}}
, and trivially:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))

The inequality
(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))
concludes this subcase, which follows from
\theta_{\ell}^{\prime}\leq\theta_{\ell}
, for all
\ell\in[1..k]
.

•

Suppose rewrite
†
‣
D.2
has length greater than 0.
The rewrite starts with either
(Step)
or
(Assm)
, depending on the top-level symbol in
t
.
The case for
(Assm)
is analogous to
(Step)
;
it merely introduces an extra conjunct that is already automaton (in which case both the rewrite of
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})
and
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau^{\prime}_{j}})({t})
will introduce it; it must be a conjunct in
U_{2,j}
and
U_{2,j}^{\prime}
).
Therefore, we assume
t=f\,\overline{{t^{\prime}}}
for some
f\in\Sigma
, so
(Step)
with some
(\forall\overline{{x_{1}}}\,\overline{{x_{2}}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x_{1}}}\,\overline{{x_{2}}}))\in V
is the first rewrite step in
†
‣
D.2
, resulting in:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}},\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}

By Lemma
D.3
,
U_{2,j}=U_{2,j,1}\land U_{2,j,2}
such that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{1}}}}}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}}])\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j,1}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j,2}
.
Let us consider both parts individually.

–

The goal formula
U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{1}}}}}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}}]
does not contain
\overline{{z}}
, so we obtain
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{1}}}}}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}}])\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j,1}
immediately from the former rewrite.

–

The goal formula
U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}]
does not contain
\overline{{y}}
, so
U_{2,j,2}=\mathsf{true}
.

Inequality
\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}\leq\overline{{\theta}}
gives us
\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})
.
We use this in combination with Lemma
D.4
to prove a sequence of implications:

\displaystyle V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta}}})({\overline{{z}}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}]

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}

We can put these two rewrites back together using
(AndL)
and
(AndR)
:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{1}}}}}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}}])\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}

All that remains is replicating the beginning of rewrite
†
‣
D.2
:

\displaystyle V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))

\displaystyle\qquad\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}},\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])

\displaystyle\qquad\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{1}}}}}[\overline{{t^{\prime}}}/\overline{{x_{1}}}])\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{\theta^{\prime}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime}_{|{\overline{{x_{2}}}}}[\overline{{z}}/\overline{{x_{2}}}])

\displaystyle\qquad\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}

We have now proved that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{i_{j}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}
determines
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{j}^{\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U_{2,j}^{\prime}
with
U_{2,j}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2,j}^{\prime}
, for all
j\in[1..m]
.
Thus, for every conjunct in
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}})({t})
, there exists a conjunct in
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})
that rewrites to something smaller, which proves
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
for a
U^{\prime}
such that
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
.
∎

Given an isomorphic type environment and automaton formula as per Definition
5.4
, we establish an equivalence between typing judgements and rewrites.
To bridge the gap, we define
\top_{o}
as a type-equivalent of truth and type goal formulas as in Figure
10
.
This typing system distills the term-typing judgements that make a goal formula true.

\begin{array}[]{cc}\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 60.77135pt\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma\vdash{t}::{q_{P}}}\mathstrut$}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathstrut$}}}\vskip-2.08893pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\raise 3.3806pt\hbox{\raise-1.1306pt\hbox{ {\small{(GT-Atom)}} }\thinspace\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{\vrule width=52.00626pt,height=0.51663pt}}}}}}\vbox{\vskip-2.08893pt\hbox{\hskip 55.66632pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{P\,t}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut$}}}}$}\par\hskip 20.0pt\par\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 49.13857pt\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma,{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G}::{\top_{o}}}\mathstrut$}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathstrut$}}}\vskip-2.08893pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\raise 3.3806pt\hbox{\raise-1.1306pt\hbox{ {\small{(GT-Imp)}} }\thinspace\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{\vrule width=101.228pt,height=0.51663pt}}}}}}\vbox{\vskip-2.08893pt\hbox{\hskip 57.12485pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U\Rightarrow G}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut$}}}}$}\par\\[12.0pt]
\hbox{$\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 50.16634pt\hbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\Gamma\vdash{G_{1}}::{\top_{o}}}\mathstrut$}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\hskip 8.00003pt{\dots}}\hskip 8.00003pt{\Gamma\vdash{G_{n}}::{\top_{o}}}\mathstrut$}}}\vskip-2.08893pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\raise 3.3806pt\hbox{\raise-1.1306pt\hbox{ {\small{(GT-And)}} }\thinspace\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{\vrule width=120.99341pt,height=0.51663pt}}}}}}\vbox{\vskip-2.08893pt\hbox{\hskip 64.62614pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{G_{1}\land\dots\land G_{n}}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut$}}}}$}\par\end{array}

Figure 10.
Typing rules for goal formulas

Lemma D.6.

For automaton formulas
U,U^{\prime}
over
\overline{{y}}
,
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
implies
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by structural induction on
U^{\prime}
.
Suppose
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
.

•

Suppose
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{true}
.
Both
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{true}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\mathsf{true}}::{\top_{o}}
trivially hold.

•

Suppose
U^{\prime}=P\,y_{i}
.
This means that
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})\leq q_{P}
and, thus,
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})
.
We aim to show
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P\,y_{i}}::{\top_{o}}
, which holds just if
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{y_{i}}::{q_{P}}
.
We prove the latter with
(T-Var)
, thanks to
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})
.

•

Suppose
U^{\prime}=(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))
for some
\overline{{x}}=x_{1}\dots x_{m}
.
This means
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})
.
By Lemma
D.1
, there exists
T\in U_{|{y_{i}}}
such that
\mathsf{fromCl}({T})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})
.

Let
T=(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{i}\,\overline{{x}}))
be such a
T
.
This gives us
\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})
by subtyping rule
(Q-Arr)
, for all
j\in[1..m]
, which implies
U^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Finally, we apply
(T-App)
to:

–

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{y_{i}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{i}\,\overline{{x}})})}
, by
(T-Var)

–

for all
j\in[1..m]
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{x_{j}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})}
, by
(T-Var)

–

for all
j\in[1..m]
:
\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime\prime}_{|{x_{j}}}})

This gives us
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{y_{i}\,\overline{{x}}}::{q_{P}}
, to which we apply
(GT-Atom)
and
(GT-Imp)
to prove the claim.

•

Suppose
U^{\prime}=U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}
.
This means that
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({(U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime})_{|{y_{i}}}})
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
Clearly, also
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{1}}^{\prime}_{|{y_{i}}}})
and
\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{z_{i}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{2}}^{\prime}_{|{y_{i}}}})
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
By the IH,
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U_{1}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.
Finally,
(GT-And)
gives us
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U_{1}^{\prime}\land U_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.

∎

Lemma D.7 (Type-clause correspondence).

For all
U
, there exists
U^{\prime}
such that
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
if, and only if
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G}::{\top_{o}}
.

Proof.

Lemma
D.6
proves
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
from
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
, so it suffices to show the following for direction
\Rightarrow
:
if
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}\triangleright G_{2}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
, then
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}}::{\top_{o}}
.

We use case analysis on the rewrite rule used in
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}\triangleright G_{2}
.

(Refl)
:

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,x\triangleright\mathsf{true}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\mathsf{true}}::{\top_{o}}
.
The side condition is
P\,x\in V
, which means
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({V_{|{x}}})
and
{x}::{q_{P}}\in\Gamma_{V}
.
Now,
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P\,x}::{\top_{o}}
can be proved using
(GT-Atom)
and
(T-Var)
.

(Step)
:

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(f\,\overline{{s}})\triangleright U^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
.
The side condition is
(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}}))\in V
, which means
\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}})})\in\mathsf{fromCl}({V_{|{f}}})
and
{f}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}})})}\in\Gamma_{V}
.

Let
U^{\prime}=T_{1}\land\dots\land T_{k}
.
Clearly, there exists a proof
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
just if
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{T_{j}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
, for all
j\in[1..k]
, using
(GT-And)
.
If
T_{j}
contains a (single) variable
x_{i}\in x_{1}\dots x_{n}
of type
\iota
, then the proof
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{T_{j}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
uses
(GT-Atom)
at the root.
Otherwise, if the (single) variable
x_{i}\in x_{1}\dots x_{n}
in
T_{j}
does not have type
\iota
, then the proof
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{T_{j}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
uses
(GT-Imp)
at the root, followed by
(GT-Atom)
;
i.e. if
T_{j}=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(x_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))
, then we have
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{T_{j}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
just if
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{P^{\prime}\,(s_{i}\,\overline{{z}})}::{\top_{o}}
.

We have now identified all
k
nodes in
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
at which
(GT-Atom)
is used.
Thanks to their premises,
T_{j}=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}(x_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))
implies
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{s_{i}\,\overline{{z}}}::{q_{P^{\prime}}}
.
Let
\overline{{z}}=z_{1}\dots z_{m}
.
By
(T-App)
, there exist
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell},{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}^{\prime}
, for all
\ell\in[1..m]
, such that:

•:

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{s_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P^{\prime}}}

•:

for all
\ell\in[1..m]
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{z_{\ell}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}^{\prime}}

•:

for all
\ell\in[1..m]
:
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}

Typing rule
(T-Var)
guarantees
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{z_{\ell}}}})
and, thus,
\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{z_{\ell}}}})\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{\ell}
.
It follows that
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P^{\prime}}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{z_{1}}}})\to\dots\to\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{z_{m}}}})\to q_{P^{\prime}}=\mathsf{fromCl}({T_{j}})
.

Let us refer to
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P^{\prime}}
as
\tau_{j}
, the type extracted from
T_{j}
for (its only variable)
x_{i}
.
Recall
\tau_{j}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({T_{j}})
.
We define an type
\theta_{i}
for each
s_{i}
such that
\theta_{i}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})
:

\displaystyle\theta_{i}

\displaystyle:=\{\tau_{j}\mid T_{j}\in U^{\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}\land j\in[1..k]\}

Finally, we have the following:

•:

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{f}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}})})}
, by
(T-Con)

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{s_{i}}::{\theta_{i}}

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
\theta_{i}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})

We conclude the case with
(T-App)
with those premises, followed by
(GT-Atom)
.

(Assm)
:

Let
\overline{{x}}=x_{1}\dots x_{n}
and
\overline{{y}}=y_{1}\dots y_{m}
.
Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{s}})\triangleright U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]\land(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y\,\overline{{x}}))
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]\land(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{x}}))}::{\top_{o}}
, for some
j\in[1..m]
.

The typing proof uses
(GT-And)
at the root with
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
on the LHS and on the RHS:

\displaystyle{\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{y_{j}\,\overline{{x}}}::{q_{P}}}\mathstrut

(GT-Atom)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{x}})}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut

(GT-Imp)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{x}})}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut

Typing rule
(T-App)
now guarantees the existence of
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i},{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}
, for all
i\in[1..n]
, such that:

•:

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{y_{j}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P}}

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{x}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{x_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}_{i}}

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}_{i}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}

The first gives us
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{j}}}})
thanks to
(T-Var)
, and the second
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})
.
This implies
\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
.
The third bullet point now yields
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}\to q_{P}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{1}}}})\to\dots\to\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{n}}}})\to q_{P}=\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{x}})})
.

The left premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{U^{\prime\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]}::{\top_{o}}
of the assumption allows us to prove the existence of
\theta_{i}
such that
\theta_{i}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{s_{i}}::{\theta_{i}}
for all
i\in[1..n]
, as we did in
(Step)
.
This means that
\theta_{i}\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}_{|{x_{i}}}})\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
.

Finally, we prove
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{s}})}::{\top_{o}}
with
(T-App)
and
(GT-Atom)
on:

•:

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{y_{j}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P}}

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{s_{i}}::{\theta_{i}}

•:

for all
i\in[1..n]
:
\theta_{i}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}

(ImpAnd)
:

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}^{\prime})\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime})\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{2}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime})\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{2}^{\prime})}::{\top_{o}}
.

Our assumption
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime})\land(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{2}^{\prime})}::{\top_{o}}
uses
(GT-And)
at the root with the subproofs given by, for
i\in[1,2]
:

\displaystyle{\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{G_{i}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}}\mathstrut

(GT-Imp)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{i}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut

Let us refer to premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{G_{i}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
as
\Delta_{i}
, for
i\in[1,2]
.

This allows us to construct:

\displaystyle{\Delta_{1}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad}\mathstrut
\displaystyle\hskip 8.00003pt{\Delta_{2}}\mathstrut

(GT-And)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime\prime}})}\vdash{G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut

(GT-Imp)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}\mathstrut

(Scope)
:

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{1}\Rightarrow G_{2})\triangleright G_{2}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
with
\overline{{z}}\cap\mathsf{FV}(G_{2})=\emptyset
.
Thanks to Weakening Lemma
D.2
,
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{1}})}\vdash{G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
also holds.
Finally,
(GT-Imp)
proves this case.

(Imp)
:

This is the first inductive case.
Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{1}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime})\triangleright(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{1}\Rightarrow G_{2}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{1}\Rightarrow G_{2}^{\prime})}::{\top_{o}}
.
The latter gives us
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{1}})}\vdash{G_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
by
(GT-Imp)
, which is equal to
\Gamma_{V\land U_{1}},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{2}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.

We use the IH on premise
V\land U_{1},\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}^{\prime}\triangleright G_{2}^{\prime}
of
(Imp)
for
\Gamma_{V\land U_{1}},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
, which is equal to
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{1}})}\vdash{G_{1}^{\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.
Finally,
(GT-Imp)
gives us
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{1}\Rightarrow G_{1}^{\prime})}::{\top_{o}}
, as required.

(AndL)
:

Suppose
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}\land G_{2}\triangleright G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
.
The proof is straightforward:
use (backwards)
(GT-And)
on
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}^{\prime}\land G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
, invoke the IH on the parts, and use
(GT-And)
for
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}\land G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
.

For direction
\Leftarrow
, suppose
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G}::{\top_{o}}
.
We use structural induction on
G
to prove
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U_{0}
for some
U_{0}
such that
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{0}
.

(GT-Atom)
:

Suppose
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P\,s}::{\top_{o}}
with premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{s}::{q_{P}}
.
We use structural induction on term
s
.

(T-Con)
:

Suppose
s=f:\iota\in\Sigma
.
By the premise,
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({V_{|{f}}})
and, thus,
P\,f\in V_{|{f}}
.
We use
(Refl)
with this side condition for:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,f\triangleright\mathsf{true}

Since
U\leq\mathsf{true}
, this proves the case.

(T-Var)
:

If
s=z\not\in\overline{{y}}
, then we proceed as in the previous case, so suppose
s=y_{j}\in\overline{{y}}
.
Clearly,
P\,y_{j}
is automaton wrt
\overline{{y}}
.
Thus,
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,y_{j}\triangleright^{*}P\,y_{j}
.

It remains to show
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}P\,y_{j}
.
Premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{y_{j}}::{q_{P}}
guarantees
q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{j}}}})
, by
(T-Var)
, which implies
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{|{y_{j}}}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}P\,y_{j}
, as required.

(T-App)
:

We must distinguish the case where
s=y_{j}\,\overline{{t}}
with
y_{j}\in\overline{{y}}
from
s=f\,\overline{{t}}
with
f\in\Sigma
, but first assume
s=f\,t_{1}\dots t_{n}=f\,\overline{{t}}
for some
f\in\Sigma
.

Premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{f\,\overline{{t}}}::{q_{P}}
guarantees
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}\to q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({V_{|{f}}})
, by
(T-App)
and
(T-Con)
, with
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}}
such that
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}_{i}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
Thus, there exists an automaton clause
(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({x_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({x_{n}})\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}}))\in V_{|{f}}
we can use as a side condition for
(Step)
in:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(f\,\overline{{t}})\triangleright\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({t_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({t_{n}})

We aim to show that each
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t_{i}})\in\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}})({t_{i}})
rewrites to an automaton clause larger than
U
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
Let us consider such a
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t_{i}})
.

•:

Suppose
t_{i}:\iota
.
Let
\tau=q_{P^{\prime}}
, so
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t_{i}})=P^{\prime}\,t_{i}
.
Recall we have
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}}
.
Since
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
implies
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}_{i}\supseteq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
for type
\iota
, we also have
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{q_{P^{\prime}}}
.

Typing rule
(GT-Atom)
gives us
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P^{\prime}\,t_{i}}::{\top_{o}}
, to which we can apply the IH for
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P^{\prime}\,t_{i}\triangleright^{*}U_{\tau}
such that
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{\tau}
.

•:

Suppose
t_{i}:\sigma_{1}\to\dots\to\sigma_{k}\to\iota
for
k>0
.
Thanks to Lemma
D.1
, the inequality
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
with
\tau\in{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
guarantees the existence of
\tau^{\prime}\in{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}
such that
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
.

Recall we have
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}}
and, thus,
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{\tau^{\prime}}
.
Let
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t_{i}})=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))
and
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau^{\prime}})({t_{i}})=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}^{\prime}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))
.
Since
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
, it holds that
U_{0}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{0}^{\prime}
.
This gives us the following:

–:

\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{0}})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}^{\prime}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}})})}
by Weakening Lemma
D.2

–:

for all
z_{\ell}\in\overline{{z}}
:
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{0}})}\vdash{z_{\ell}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{0}}_{|{z_{\ell}}}})}
, by
(T-Var)

–:

for all
z_{\ell}\in\overline{{z}}
:
\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{0}}_{|{z_{\ell}}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{U_{0}^{\prime}}_{|{z_{\ell}}}})

We apply
(T-Var)
to these premises for
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{0}})}\vdash{t_{i}\,\overline{{z}}}::{q_{P^{\prime}}}
.
By
(GT-Atom)
,
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{0}})}\vdash{P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}})}::{\top_{o}}
, which is equal to
\Gamma_{V\land U_{0}},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}})}::{\top_{o}}
.

Finally, by the IH, there exists
U_{\tau}
such that
V\land U_{0},\overline{{y}}\vdash P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}})\triangleright^{*}U_{\tau}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{\tau}
.
Typing rule
(GT-Imp)
gives us
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow U_{\tau})
.
Observe that
U_{\tau}
is automaton wrt
\overline{{y}}
.
Therefore, it does not contain
\overline{{z}}
, and we can apply
(Scope)
for:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow P^{\prime}\,(t_{i}\,\overline{{z}}))\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow U_{\tau})\triangleright U_{\tau}

We have proved, for all
i\in[1..n]
, that every
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t_{i}})\in\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}})({t_{i}})
rewrites to some automaton clause greater than
U
.
Therefore, so does
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}})({t_{i}})
, and so does
\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({t_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({t_{n}})
.
We conclude the case with:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(f\,\overline{{t}})\triangleright\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({t_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({t_{n}})

Now, suppose
s=y_{j}\,t_{1}\dots t_{n}
with
y_{j}\in\overline{{y}}
instead.
Similar to before, premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{y_{j}\,\overline{{t}}}::{q_{P}}
guarantees
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}\to q_{P}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({U_{|{y_{j}}}})
, by
(T-App)
and
(T-Var)
, with
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t_{i}}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}^{\prime}}
such that
{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}^{\prime}_{i}\leq{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{i}
, for all
i\in[1..n]
.
Thus, there exists an automaton clause
T=(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({x_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({x_{n}})\Rightarrow P\,(y_{j}\,\overline{{x}}))\in U_{|{y_{j}}}
we can use as assumption in
(Assm)
:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,(f\,\overline{{t}})\triangleright\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}})({t_{1}})\land\dots\land\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{n}})({t_{n}})\land T

Conjunct
T
is in
U
by definition.
For the other conjuncts, we proceed as before.

(GT-And)
:

Suppose
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}\land G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
with premises
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{1}}::{\top_{o}}
and
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G_{2}}::{\top_{o}}
.
By the IH, there exist
U_{1},U_{2}
such that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}\triangleright^{*}U_{1}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{2}\triangleright^{*}U_{2}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{2}
.
Clearly,
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{1}\land U_{2}
, so the following rewrite proves the case, using
(AndL)
and
(AndR)
:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G_{1}\land G_{2}\triangleright^{*}U_{1}\land G_{2}\triangleright^{*}U_{1}\land U_{2}

(GT-Imp)
:

Suppose
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G^{\prime\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
with premise
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})},{\overline{{z}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U^{\prime}})}\vdash{G^{\prime\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
, which is equal to
\Gamma_{V\land U^{\prime}},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{G^{\prime\prime}}::{\top_{o}}
.
By the IH, there exists
U^{\prime\prime}
such that
V\land U^{\prime},\overline{{y}}\vdash G^{\prime\prime}\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime\prime}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}
.
Rewrite rule
(Imp)
gives us
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G^{\prime\prime})\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime})
.

It remains to show this rewrites further to something larger than
U
.
Observe that
U^{\prime\prime}
is automaton wrt
\overline{{y}}
.
Therefore, it does not contain
\overline{{z}}
, and we can apply
(Scope)
for:

V,\overline{{y}}\vdash(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow G^{\prime\prime})\triangleright^{*}(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,U^{\prime}\Rightarrow U^{\prime\prime})\triangleright U^{\prime\prime}

∎

See
5.6

Proof.

We aim to show
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}
if, and only if,
\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})}::{\top_{o}}
;
this proves
(i)
, since Lemma
D.7
already gives us
\exists U^{\prime}.\,U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}\land V,\overline{{y}}\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
if, and only if
\Gamma_{V},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})}::{\top_{o}}
, for all
U
.
Furthermore, if
\lvert\overline{{y}}\rvert=0
, then
U=\mathsf{true}
and
U^{\prime}=\mathsf{true}
, so
(ii)
is an instance of
(i)
.

It remains to prove
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}
if, and only if,
\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})}::{\top_{o}}
.

Suppose
t:\iota
.
This yields the following sequence of equivalences:

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{t}::{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}

\displaystyle\iff\forall q_{P}\in{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}.\,\Gamma\vdash{t}::{q_{P}}

\displaystyle\iff\forall q_{P}\in{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}.\,\Gamma\vdash{P\,t}::{\top_{o}}

\displaystyle\iff\forall q_{P}\in{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}.\,\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({q_{P}})({t})}::{\top_{o}}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}})({t})}::{\top_{o}}

where we rely on notation,
(GT-Atom)
,
\mathsf{toCl}({q_{P}})({t})=P\,t
, and
(GT-And)
, resp.

Suppose
t:\sigma_{1}\to\dots\to\sigma_{m}\to\iota
with
m>0
.
It suffices to prove the claim for a strict type
\tau
of this type, thanks to
(GT-And)
.
Let
\tau={\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{1}\to\dots\to{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}_{m}\to q_{P}
.
We obtain the following equivalences:

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma,{\overline{{z}}}::{\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}}\vdash{t}::{\tau}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma,{\overline{{z}}}::{\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}}\vdash{t\,\overline{{z}}}::{q_{P}}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma,{\overline{{z}}}::{\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}}\vdash{P\,(t\,\overline{{z}})}::{\top_{o}}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma\vdash{\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}})}::{\top_{o}}

\displaystyle\iff\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t})}::{\top_{o}}

where we respectively rely on Weakening Lemma
D.2
,
(T-App)
,
(GT-Atom)
,
(GT-Imp)
, and
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t})=(\forall\overline{{z}}.\,\mathsf{toCl}({\overline{{{\sigma_{\mathsf{t}}}}}})({\overline{{z}}})\Rightarrow P\,(t\,\overline{{z}}))
.

∎

D.3.
Reducing HORS intersection typing to
MSL
(
\omega
)

Lemma D.8.

A non-empty
\Gamma
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent if, and only if, there exist
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in V_{\Gamma}
and
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,P\,t\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in D_{\mathcal{G}}
such that:

\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}\text{ is }{\mathcal{G}}\text{-coconsistent and }\exists U^{\prime}.\,V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}},\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,t\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}\land U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}

where
\tau=\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}})})
.

Proof.

Take the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistency for non-empty
\Gamma
, and replace parts of the statement by equivalent ones, relying on the isomorphism between types and clauses, the construction of
D_{\mathcal{G}}
, and Proposition
5.6
, resp.:

•

{f}::{\tau}\in\Gamma
if, and only if,
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in V_{\Gamma}

•

\exists(f\,\overline{{y}}=t)\in{\mathcal{G}}
if, and only if,
\exists(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,P\,t\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in D_{\mathcal{G}}

•

\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\},{\overline{{y}}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({U})}\vdash{t}::{q_{P}}
if, and only if,
\exists U^{\prime}.\,V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}},\overline{{y}}\vdash P\,t\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}\land U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}

∎

Lemma D.9.

(1)

\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent

(2)

If
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U
, then there exists
U^{\prime}
such that
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
and
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
.

(3)

If
V_{1}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}V_{2}
, then
V_{2}\subseteq{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V_{1})
.

(4)

If
\Gamma
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent, then
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}V_{\Gamma}
.

(5)

If
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}
and
\Gamma
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent, then there exists
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
such that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}\vdash{t}::{\tau^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

We prove each part separately.

(1)

Type environment
\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}
is constructed inductively in parallel to an automaton approximation, which means there exists a linear order of environments
\Gamma_{0},\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{n}
where
\Gamma_{0}=\emptyset
and
\Gamma_{n}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}
and
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{i-1}\uplus\{{f_{i}}::{\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U_{i}\Rightarrow P_{i}\,(f_{i}\,\overline{{y}})})}\}
, for all
1\leq i\leq n
, such that there exists
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,G_{i}\Rightarrow P_{i}\,(f_{i}\,\overline{{y}}))\in D_{\mathcal{G}}
and
V_{\Gamma_{i}}\vdash G_{i}\triangleright^{*}U_{i}
.

By a straightforward induction with Lemma
D.8
, each
\Gamma_{i}
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent.
Therefore, so is
\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}
.

(2)

Suppose
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U
.
Clearly,
V\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V)
.
Starting from the end of the rewrite, we inductively replace each application of
(Step)
with a
T^{\prime}\in{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V)
by
(Step)
with a smaller
T\in V
, which exists by Subtyping Lemma
D.1
.
(We do not need to consider
(Refl)
, because
P\,x\in{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V)
if and only if
P\,x\in V
.)

Suppose
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright G^{\prime}\triangleright^{n}U
where
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright G^{\prime}
is the only step to use an automaton clause from
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V)\backslash V
.
Let
T^{\prime}=(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U_{0}^{\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}}))\in{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V)
be the side condition, so
G=P\,(f\,\overline{{s}})
and
G^{\prime}=U_{0}^{\prime}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]
.
Let
T=(\forall\overline{{x}}.\,U_{0}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{x}}))\in V
such that
T\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}T^{\prime}
.
This means
U_{0}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U_{0}
.

By monotonicity of rewrite (Lemma
D.5
), there exists
U^{\prime}
such that
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}
and
V,\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright U_{0}[\overline{{s}}/\overline{{x}}]\triangleright^{n}U^{\prime}
.

(3)

Suppose
V_{1}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}V_{2}
over variables
\overline{{y}}
.
By definition, for all
y\in\overline{{y}}
,
\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{1}}_{|{y}}})\leq\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{2}}_{|{y}}})
.
By Subtyping Lemma
D.1
, for every
\tau_{2}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{2}}_{|{y}}})
, there exists
\tau_{1}\in\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{1}}_{|{y}}})
such that
\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, which is
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{1}})({y})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{2}})({y})
in the clause setting.

Clearly,
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{2}})({y})\in{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{1}})({y}))
.
It follows from
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{1}})({y})\in\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{1}}_{|{y}}})
and
\mathsf{toCl}({\tau_{2}})({y})\in\mathsf{fromCl}({{V_{2}}_{|{y}}})
that
V_{2}\subseteq{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(V_{1})
, as required.

(4)

We use induction on the size of
\Gamma
.
If
\Gamma
is empty, then
V_{\Gamma}=\emptyset\subseteq\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})
, so suppose
\Gamma
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent and non-empty.

As per Lemma
D.8
, let
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in V_{\Gamma}
and
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,G\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in D_{\mathcal{G}}
such that:

\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}\text{ is }{\mathcal{G}}\text{-coconsistent and }\exists U^{\prime}.\,V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}},\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}\land U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime}

where
\tau=\mathsf{fromCl}({\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}})})
.

By the IH,
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}}
.
Now,
3
gives us
V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}}\subseteq{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}}))
.
Thus, rewrite
V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}},\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
gives us
{\uparrow_{\mathsf{a}}}(\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime}
.
By
2
,
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}}),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime\prime}
for some
U^{\prime\prime}
such that
U^{\prime}\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}
.
Transitivity yields
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}
.

Since
V_{\Gamma}=V_{\Gamma\backslash\{{f}::{\tau}\}}\land\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({f})
, it remains to show that
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({f})
, which is
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}})
.
Now,
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,G\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in D_{\mathcal{G}}
and
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}}),\overline{{y}}\vdash G\triangleright^{*}U^{\prime\prime}
satisfy the side conditions of the rewrite algorithm for including
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))
in
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})
.

Finally,
U\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}U^{\prime\prime}
implies
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))
, so
(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))\in\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})
gives us
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}(\forall\overline{{y}}.\,U\Rightarrow P\,(f\,\overline{{y}}))
, which concludes the proof.

(5)

Suppose
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}
and
\Gamma
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent.
By
4
,
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\mathrel{\leq_{\mathsf{a}}}V_{\Gamma}
, which means
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}\leq\Gamma_{V_{\Gamma}}=\Gamma
.
By Lemma 3.2.3 from
Ramsay 2014
, there exists
\tau^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}\vdash{t}::{\tau^{\prime}}
and
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
.

∎

See
5.9

Proof.

We prove the following:

\exists{\mathcal{G}}\text{-coconsistent }\Gamma.\,\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}\text{ if, and only if, }\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}

For direction
\Leftarrow
, suppose
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
Since
\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}
, Proposition
5.6
gives us
\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\vdash{t}::{\tau}
.
We know from
1
that
\Gamma^{\infty}(D_{\mathcal{G}})
is
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent, which proves the claim.

For direction
\Rightarrow
, suppose there exists a
{\mathcal{G}}
-coconsistent
\Gamma
such that
\Gamma\vdash{t}::{\tau}
.
By
5
, there exists
\tau^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})}\vdash{t}::{\tau^{\prime}}
and
\tau^{\prime}\leq\tau
.
Proposition
5.6
gives us
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau^{\prime}})({t})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
Finally, Lemma
D.5
gives us
\mathcal{V}(D_{\mathcal{G}})\vdash\mathsf{toCl}({\tau})({t})\triangleright^{*}\mathsf{true}
.
∎
